# Objective problem map — NSSCAS Mathematics 8227

family-exam candidate comparisons complete; other R7 gates reported separately; independent check required

Every objective retains all its demands. A missing exam match never removes textbook or Cambridge practice.

```json
{
  "by_objective": {
    "total": 60,
    "with_exam_parts": 59,
    "with_usable_exam_parts": 58,
    "with_same_type_textbook_practice": 45,
    "with_only_variant_or_other_practice": 15,
    "with_no_practice_anywhere": 0,
    "with_unfinished_textbook_comparison": 0
  },
  "by_demand": {
    "total": 138,
    "with_exam_parts": 127,
    "with_usable_exam_parts": 126,
    "with_same_type_textbook_practice": 73,
    "with_only_variant_or_other_practice": 64,
    "with_no_practice_anywhere": 1,
    "with_unfinished_textbook_comparison": 0
  }
}
```

Same-type and close-variant counts use recorded family relations only. Other practice includes unfinished comparisons, explicitly labelled. No-practice-anywhere means no usable exam part, textbook family, accepted item-union support or recorded Cambridge item. Counts overlap; an objective counts once when any constituent demand qualifies.

Same-type and structural-branch counts are lower bounds. Roughly one in five or six sampled rejections was judged related; these small samples do not estimate a population error rate. In step 3 search every family member and the accepted item-level assessed/component union links (R5-03), not only recorded relations. A rejection means relation not established; a templated rejection reason is not source comparison evidence. R5-03: source-item support uses the union of accepted assessed/component links and is a lower bound. These links do not establish equivalence for every part of a connected item. Same-type and structural-branch entries identify the actual exemplar responses; A relation explicitly marked supporting assessed subtask does not cover the complete exam task. the full family member list is context and does not certify every member against that exam part. Held exam parts and intended-type comparisons are internal defective evidence, excluded from same-type/branch practice counts and usable anchors. Textbook originals remain internal. This map is not a PLAN, approved recipe bank or learner deck.

## 8227:2020:M1:T1:O02

Find the discriminant of a quadratic polynomial $ax^2+bx+c$ and interpret the nature and number of real roots of the related equation $f(x)=ax^2+bx+c$.

### 8227:2020:M1:T1:O02:D01 — Calculate discriminant

examination-observed

NSSCAS: 2021 P1 Q10(a); 2021 P1 Q10(b); 2022 P1 Q4(answer); 2023 P1 Q1(b) [held]; 2024 P1 Q5(a); 2024 P1 Q5(b); 2025 P1 Q1(a).

Same type: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-nature-without-solving → 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q5:b; exemplar 8227:TB:YM:C1:Try-Now:14 [b], PDF 27; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-universal-proof → 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:b; exemplar 8227:TB:YM:C1:Try-Now:20 [b-universal-real-proof], PDF 30; 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-universal-proof → 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q1:a; exemplar 8227:TB:YM:C1:Try-Now:19 [a-integer-proof], PDF 30.

Close variants: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-nature-without-solving → 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:a; The source classifies fixed numerical coefficients; the exam first eliminates a line against a curve and solves a discriminant inequality for all non-intersecting slopes. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-nature-without-solving → 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q10:b; The source computes a numerical discriminant; the exam proves its sign for every non-zero parameter, rather than classifying one numerical equation. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-nature-without-solving → 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q4:answer; The source reports a fixed root classification; the exam reverses the condition to solve a two-sided parameter bound involving k-squared. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-nature-without-solving → 8227:EXAM:2024:P1:Q5:a; The source gives a numerical classification; the exam determines the entire parameter interval for a negative discriminant. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-nature-without-solving → 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q1:a; The source checks a numerical square discriminant; the exam must establish a symbolic perfect square and rational roots for every admitted integer k. (easier).

Other textbook practice: none recorded.

## 8227:2020:M1:T1:O04

Recognise and solve equations in $x$ which are quadratic in some function of $x$, e.g. $x^4-5x^2+4=0$ with $k=x^2$.

### 8227:2020:M1:T1:O04:D01 — Recognise quadratic substitution

examination-observed

NSSCAS: 2021 P1 Q3(a); 2021 P1 Q3(b); 2021 P2 Q11(b); 2022 P1 Q2(b); 2022 P2 Q2(answer); 2024 P1 Q1(answer); 2025 P1 Q11(b).

Same type: relation not established for the recorded exemplars.

Close variants: 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-transformation-recovery → 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q3:a; The source quartic has a nonzero x-squared term and requires solving a genuine quadratic in x-squared; the exam binomial x⁴-81 can be solved by direct fourth-root/difference-of-squares reasoning. The source adds the intermediate quadratic decision. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-transformation-recovery → 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q11:b; The source Try Now12(a) is exactly the same quartic x⁴-13x²+36. It asks only for its real roots; the exam additionally selects the positive roots as the labelled intersection abscissae a and b. The source lacks that final graphical selection. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-square-root-recovery → 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q3:b; The source solves its quadratic in the principal square root without an earlier supplied root set and must reject a negative intermediate root. The exam reuses the fourth-power roots from (a) before undoing the shifted square root; both shifted candidates are admissible. The source adds independent solution and rejection evidence. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-square-root-recovery → 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q2:b; Both equations are quadratic in the principal square root and require rejecting a negative intermediate root. The source derives and solves that quadratic; the exam says hence and supplies the relevant quadratic roots in (a), reducing the independent work. (harder); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-affine-recovery-chain → 8227:EXAM:2021:P1:Q3:b; The source recovers y from 2y-3 using established roots, a one-to-one affine inverse. The exam must undo a shifted principal square root and check its admissibility; the source has no sign filter. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-affine-recovery-chain → 8227:EXAM:2021:P2:Q11:b; The source uses an affine substitution into an already solved quadratic; the exam embeds exponential recovery in logarithm/domain conditions. Those additional restrictions are absent from the source. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-affine-recovery-chain → 8227:EXAM:2022:P1:Q2:b; The source affine inverse accepts every real intermediate root; the exam principal square root must reject the negative quadratic root before squaring. (easier); 8227:TEXTBOOK-FAMILY:quadratic-affine-recovery-chain → 8227:EXAM:2025:P1:Q11:b; The source affine inverse returns one value per base root; the exam x-squared inverse branches into signed roots and then selects positive graph coordinates. (easier).

## 8227:2020:M1:T6:O06

Find solutions in degrees or radians within a specified interval of $\sin(nx)=k$, $\cos(nx)=k$, $\tan(nx)=k$, and equations easily reducible to these forms, where $n$ is a small integer or simple fraction. General forms of solution are not included.

## 8227:2020:M1:T9:O04

use definite integration to find: - the area of a region bounded by a curve and a line, or between two curves - a volume of revolution about one of the axes - displacement, velocity and acceleration

## 8227:2020:M2:T1:O01

Interpret and use $|x|$, and use relations such as $|a|=|b|\Leftrightarrow a^2=b^2$ and $|x-a|<b\Leftrightarrow a-b<x<a+b$ in solving equations and inequalities. The source prints an extra equals sign in the first relation; this normalization removes that typographical error.

## 8227:2020:M2:T5:O01

Use reverse differentiation to integrate $e^{ax+b}$, $1/(ax+b)$, $\sin(ax+b)$, $\cos(ax+b)$ and $\sec^2(ax+b)$. General integration by substitution is not required.
